<meta>This page serves as an introduction or "preliminary report" to a symbolic engraving (the "Figure") found in a collection of works by the Christian mystic Jacob Böhme. The text explains the use of allegories and symbols in spiritual literature.</meta>

# Preliminary Report regarding the Figure.

AS all Holy Scripture—which flowed from God’s mouth through his holy teachers, prophets, and apostles—consists almost entirely of figures, hidden prophetic speeches, riddles, and stories (besides the single instructional goal of repentance and godliness), in these the wonders of Divine Wisdom truly appear: they tell of the past, prefigure the present, and indicate the future. However, they are structured so that neither coarse, carnal reason, nor self-wise, clever reason can understand or guess them at the first encounter. In this way, the hidden nature of Divine Wisdom remains in its dignity, and the wise and understanding heart is given cause to dig and search for wisdom. Just as, externally, the beautiful and shining gold—the noblest body or essence of all nature—is dug out of the dark and coarse earth according to Solomon's parable in *Proverbs 3, verse 14*:

So one finds in these writings of Divine Revelation such a depth and wealth of wisdom that all reason sinks before it. One need only consider the <term>Philosophical Sphere</term> <note>original: "Philosophische Kugel"</note> or the <term>Wonder-Eye of Eternity</term> <note>original: "Wunder-Auge der Ewigkeit"</note> in the *Forty Questions*, page 272; also the Wheel of Nature in *The Threefold Life*, page 153; and then the secret interpretation of the End of Times in the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterio Magno"</note>, page 241. This is not to mention what remains wrapped up here and there throughout the writings.

From these, through Divine Grace, we have caught sight of the ideal form of this spirit—as the gift of revelation for this time—and presented it to the lovers of wisdom for further reflection in the first figure on the title page of all his works. An interpretation of the same is as impossible to provide in this brevity as it is unnecessary to include, because it is explained abundantly everywhere in the writings; we direct the reader there, having cleared the path with references found throughout all <term>treatises</term>, <term>chapters</term>, and <term>verses</term>.

However, to touch upon it only slightly, we wish to remind the reader of what the author <note>Referring to Jacob Böhme (1575–1624), the mystic whose works this volume contains.</note> discusses everywhere regarding the two eternal <term>principles</term> <note>original: "Principien"</note> of the worlds of Darkness and Light: that they indeed form one sphere in <term>Eternity</term> <note>original: "Æternität"</note>, and yet in their quality they stand as separate and back-to-back against each other.

The black semicircle on the left is the <term>principle</term> of the dark world; the subtle one on the right, that of the Light-World. The middle one, like a clock-wheel, is the outer <term>mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium"</note> of the <term>creation</term> <note>original: "Creation"</note> of this world, or the third <term>principle</term>. The seven circles around it, where the seventh appears at the top like a shining sun—

net/ <note>This is a catchword, the first syllable of the first word on the following page, "erscheinet" (appears).</note>

## All Theosophical <unclear>Writings</unclear> <note>original: "Alle Theosophischen Schriften." The term <term>Theosophical</term> refers to Böhme’s specific brand of "divine wisdom," a mystical philosophy seeking to understand the nature of God and the creation of the universe through direct spiritual insight.</note>

<meta>This page follows the title page and its blank reverse. It serves as a preface or "Preliminary Report" explaining the complex symbolic engraving (the "Figure") that often accompanied the collected editions of Jacob Böhme’s works.</meta>

## Preliminary Report concerning the Figure.

**J**ust as the whole Holy Scripture—which flowed from God’s mouth through his holy teachers, prophets, and apostles—consists almost entirely of figures, hidden prophetic speeches, riddles, and stories (besides the single instructional purpose of repentance and godliness), in which the wonders of Divine Wisdom truly appear, such that they recount the past, prefigure the present, and indicate the future; yet they do so in such a way that neither coarse, fleshly reason nor self-conceited, "wise-in-its-own-eyes" reason can understand or guess such things at the first approach. For in this way, the hiddenness of Divine Wisdom remains in its dignity, and only the wise and understanding heart is given cause to dig and search for wisdom. Just as externally the beautiful and shining gold, as the noblest body or essence of all nature, is dug out of the dark and coarse earth, according to Solomon’s proverb, Proverbs 3:14. <note>Proverbs 3:14: "For the merchandise of it is better than the merchandise of silver, and the gain thereof than fine gold."</note>

So one finds in these writings of Divine Revelation such a depth and wealth of wisdom, before which all reason sinks away; one should only consider the <term>Philosophical Sphere</term> <note>Also known as the "Philosophical Globe," a diagram representing Böhme's view of the universe.</note> or the "Wonder-Eye of Eternity" in the *Forty Questions* <note>Referring to Böhme's 1620 work, *Forty Questions on the Soul*.</note>, page 272; likewise the "Wheel of Nature" in the *Threefold Life* <note>Referring to *The High and Deep Searching of the Threefold Life of Man*, 1620.</note>, page 153; and then the secret interpretation of the "End of Times" in the *Mysterium Magnum* <note>Böhme's massive commentary on Genesis, 1623.</note>, page 241. This is without even mentioning what remains wrapped up here and there within the writings.

From these, we have, through Divine Grace, glimpsed the ideal form of this spirit—as the gift of revelation for this time—and presented it to the lovers of wisdom for further reflection in the first figure on the title page of all his works. An interpretation of this is as impossible to provide in this brevity as it is unnecessary to set down, because it is explained abundantly everywhere throughout the writings; we direct the reader there, having paved the way with the listed citations through all treatises, chapters, and verses.

However, to touch upon it only slightly, we wish to remind the reader of what the author discusses everywhere regarding the two eternal principles of the Dark-world and Light-world: that they indeed form a sphere in <term>Eternity</term> <note>original: "Æternität"</note>, and yet in their properties stand as if separated and back-to-back against one another.

The black half-circle on the left is the Principle of the dark world; the subtle one on the right is the light world. The middle one, like a clock-gear, is the outer Mystery of the creation of this world, or the Third Principle. The 7 circles around it, where the 7th at the top appears as a shining sun [appears]...

<meta>catchword: appears /</meta>

# Preliminary Report concerning the Figure.

...[have] different interpretations. As the seven spirits of **God** / or seven properties of nature. The seven days of the world's creation with the Sabbath. The seven seals; the seven times / and the seven petitions in the Our Father / etc. <note>These "sevens" refer to the seven properties of eternal nature, a core concept in Böhme's cosmology where the number seven represents the completed manifestation of God's spirit.</note>

12. So also the twelve numbers of the hours are indicated in the **Scriptures**, wherein this is to be noted. The number 1, which stands at the top outside the circle in the <term>Vertex</term> <note>original: "Vertice"; the highest point or zenith of the diagram.</note> or crown-point of the clock-circle / signifies firstly the Divine **Unity** / as the beginning of all beings / or the Majesty with the crown and scepter / without change or motion / and incomprehensible to the creature / because it is a spirit. Downwards through the † <note>The cross symbol represents the intersection of the divine and temporal planes.</note> to the number 7 / designates the <term>Essentiality</term> <note>In Böhmean terms, this refers to "Divine Substantiality" or the spiritual "body" of God.</note> or the holy element of the Divine Corporality / out of which all things are created. Secondly, it signifies the **First Single Man Adam** / whom **GOD** created in the glorified body of light or paradisiacal property / and set as King or ruler of all creatures / as the 8th Psalm says: You have put all things under his feet. But because he left the **Unity of God** / as the true Wisdom / and lusted after the cleverness of **Knowledge** <note>original: "Erkäntnuß"; specifically the "Knowledge of Good and Evil."</note> / where evil and good are mixed / so also the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"; the animating spirit of the physical, temporal universe.</note> seized him and led him into the changeable wheel of time and vanity / so that he fell from his height and dignity; just as the *Spiritus Mundi* even at this hour and until the end of time / publicly depicts this on all clock-circles in the world with this ♄ **Character** <note>The astrological symbol for Saturn, representing time, lead, and the melancholy or "contractive" force in Böhme's signature of things.</note> / though it is considered or understood by no one.

Thirdly, it signifies the name **JESUS**, as the highest love of the **Unity of God** / who gave himself into the death of fallen man / and birthed him again to life; he has conquered again the lost kingdom of man / has **Ascended** to heaven / and has taken up again the royal <term>Place</term> <note>original: "Locum"</note> of **Paradise** / reigns now in the spirit of the believers / and will, after the completion of time, reign with them as their King and Divine Monarch of Peace into all eternity.

Fourthly, there are more other interpretations of the types of **Christ** in the patriarchs Isaac / Jacob / Judah / Joseph etc. / which are to be found in their designated places.

The following circle to the left hand downwards / where the number

# Preliminary Report concerning the Figure.

2. and 3. enclosed / are separated from the uppermost-brightly-shining Circle / so that it does not touch it / which already indicates, alongside what follows, the divided or separated property of fallen Human Nature / namely the reality of malice / as seen in Cain, the second human / who set himself above the third, his brother Abel, out of pride / ambition and envy / and murdered him / thus this generation took possession of the world through the sword of vengeance / and through all ages has become increasingly more fruitful in the curse upon earth.

But that the rays of the uppermost Circle still penetrate this / indicates the embodied Word of Grace in the covenant of the promise of the <term>Crusher of the Serpent</term> <note>original: "Schlangen-tretter"; a reference to the prophecy in Genesis 3:15, interpreted here as the coming of Christ.</note> / as the holy and pious mind of Abel / and all succeeding holy <term>Patriarchs</term> <note>original: "Erzt-vätter"; literally "Arch-fathers."</note> / teachers and prophets.

The Eye in the center-point of the clockwork and the pointer in the fiery triangle, enclosed by a three-fold watery triangle / and surrounded by a shine of light / indicates the Eye of **GOD** / and also the eye of man in the <term>Holy Ternary</term> <note>original: "Ternario Sancto"; refers to the three-fold manifestation of God within the created order.</note> / through which **GOD** sees / and man reveals the mysteries of **GOD** and His will according to the form of this time / how close the time has run to its end / and herewith the seventh trumpet is blown / so that the mystery of the Kingdom of **GOD** shall be completed and made manifest / when Babel is swallowed up by the fiery sword of the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turbæ"; in Böhmean theosophy, the "Turba" is the spirit of wrath, or the awakening of the dark principle that destroys vanity and false systems (Babel).</note> / and all the curse of vanity is consumed.

The Alphabet around the inside / designates the revealed <term>Language of Nature</term> <note>original: "Natur-Sprache"; the mystical idea that names should perfectly express the inner essence of things, a language lost after the Fall or the Tower of Babel.</note> / concerning which the wonders of **GOD** are sufficiently discussed in the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterio Magno"; a reference to one of Jacob Böhme's major works, an exposition on Genesis.</note> and other indicated places / which is not necessary to elaborate here.

And just as this first [figure] is touched upon here only in the briefest manner / so we wish to similarly point out the actual interpretations in the following special figures of each book according to the title / where the power of the Word will reveal more to the God-loving reader and penetrate the heart / than this slight testimony or ornament / which we nevertheless have received as a gift out of God’s love / and have drawn from this fountain / but have added for the reader only as a thoughtful sight for the sake of further inquiry.

Wherein this little bit is still to be remembered: That although the form of the Eternal Divine Birth and Revelation through the two <term>Principles</term> <note>original: "Principia"; the fundamental forces of Light/Love and Darkness/Wrath.</note> of Darkness and Light / together with everything that is Spiritual and hidden / cannot be depicted: For / that which is in one another and must remain / and yet one [is] to the...

<meta>This page continues the "Preliminary Report" concerning a mystical diagram. The previous page discussed the symbolism of the "Eye of God" and the "Word of Grace." # Preliminary Report of the Figure.

...to others incomprehensible, inconceivable, and unmixed, or separated by a chasm of an entirely different birth; it has no communion with any formation. However, according to the degree of **preeminence** <note>original: "Præminenz"; meaning superiority or excellence.</note> or the excellence of the Good, all formation is made through the ✚ <note>The cross symbol represents the intersection of the divine and the material, a central concept in Böhme’s "Signature of All Things."</note>, which is the main foundation of all <term>signature</term> <note>original: "Signatur"; the outward mark or form that reveals the inner essence of a thing.</note> or designation of the eternal, divine, and temporal birth of all beings, in **up** and **down**, in **above** and **below**, in **light** and **darkness**, in **spirit** and **body**, in **life** and **death**, in **fire** and **water**, in **air** and **earth**, etc.

Finally, it is also to be noted regarding the **author's** <note>original: "Authoris"</note> own figure of the <term>Philosophical Sphere</term> <note>original: "Philosophischen Kugel"; a diagram used to explain the interaction of divine principles.</note> in the *Forty Questions* <note>Referring to Jacob Böhme’s 1620 work, "Forty Questions on the Soul."</note>, that there the **principles** <note>original: "Principia"</note> are **located** <note>original: "locirt"</note> or placed in reverse. Whereas the first **principle** <note>original: "Principium"</note> of darkness should stand on the left hand—according to the form of the order of the Last Judgment—but here it stands on the right, and the light stands on the left. This was not set out of error, but rather because of the order of reading for the sake of the inserted names or words, which, according to the writing style of the German language, are read away from oneself or outwards, and not (as with the Jews) <note>A reference to the Hebrew language, which is read right-to-left.</note> inwards. But he who grasps the meaning in his understanding can easily concede this; otherwise, the **author** has placed them correctly in the table sent to **Mr. Gottfried Freudenhammer** <note>A physician and close friend of Jacob Böhme who received several of his "Theosophic Epistles."</note> with his 47th **epistle** <note>original: "Send-brieff".</note>.

# Address

## To the

### R E A D E R.

**Friendly, kind-hearted, and impartial reader.**

<note>The text begins with a large decorative initial 'E' (standing for "Es," or "It"), featuring a woodcut design of ornate floral and vine motifs.</note> It is right that a precious and dear treasure be brought into faithful protection, so that it may be received according to its dignity and be well-preserved.

Now, one finds gold, silver, and precious stones in the houses of kings and princes; and those who hunt after wealth <note>original: "Reichthumb"</note> also capture their share and lock it in their chests, so that they may enjoy it in times of need and also appear heartily with it in times of peace.

The <term>Kingdom of God<gloss>Reich GOttes; in Böhme's theology, this refers not to a physical place but to the internal, spiritual reality of the soul where the divine light dwells.</gloss></term> however, is found neither with Herod, Pilate <note>original: "Herode, Pilato"; Böhme uses the Latinized forms of these biblical names associated with the trial of Jesus.</note>, nor Caiaphas <note>original: "Caipha"; the High Priest who presided over the Sanhedrin trials.</note>, but rather among the innocent <note>original: "unmündigen"; literally "under-age" or "minor," referring to those who are spiritually humble and childlike rather than worldly-wise.</note> children and simple hearts, who—although they possess neither power—

<summary>This page introduces the book's preface, framing spiritual wisdom as a "precious treasure" that must be protected. It argues that divine truth is found not among the powerful and wealthy, but within the hearts of the simple and humble.</summary>
<keywords>preface, spiritual treasure, Kingdom of God, humility, simplicity, Herod, Pilate, Caiaphas, Jakob Böhme</keywords>

# Commission

—armed with power and strength, nor gifted with cleverness and great reason, nor clothed with honor, dignity, and prestige, nor provided with wealth <note>original: "Reichthumb"</note>; yet chosen by God so that He might set up His tabernacle <note>original: "Hütte"; a reference to the dwelling place of God among humans, as in the biblical Tabernacle or "tent" of meeting.</note> within them, and entrust to them His power, His Holy Spirit, His divine <term>essence<gloss>Wesenheit; in Böhme’s theology, this refers to the actual spiritual substance or "being-ness" of God that manifests within the soul.</gloss></term>, His light, His wisdom—yes, even His mysteries—and lay His heart into their hearts, so that they may preserve it, and thereby also be preserved and proven.

It is not necessary to prove this at great length, since the whole of Holy Scripture—with its histories, deeds, and words—testifies enough as to where and in what kind of vessels God is accustomed to placing His treasures: namely, the Word into the flesh, Christ into the cattle stall, the little pearl into the field, the Kingdom of God into the little children, and the mysteries <note>original: "Geheimnußse"</note> into the simple apostles, who could either not read and write at all, or only barely. Concerning this, Christ Himself thanks His Heavenly Father, and His chosen instrument <note>original: "Rüstzeug"; literally "tool" or "equipment," a common term for Paul as the "chosen vessel."</note> Paul chimes in from his own experience when he says: *Not many wise according to the flesh, not many powerful, not many noble are called, but what is foolish and*

## To the Reader.

> —what is **despised** <note>original: "veracht"; this word completes the sentence from the previous page.</note>, that has He chosen, so that He might put the wise to shame; and what is weak before the world, that has **God** chosen, that He might put to shame what is strong; and the ignoble before the world and the despised has **God** chosen, and that which is nothing, that He might bring to nothing what is something, so that no flesh may boast before Him.

These cited words of Paul, along with many others consistent with Holy Scripture, have now grown old through the passage of time; they have attained the reputation and belief that **God** spoke them, and they are also truly and rightly called the words of **God**, because they flowed from the Spirit of Christ that was in him, according to his own explanation: "We have not received the spirit of the world, but the Spirit from **God**, that we may know what has been given to us by **God**, which we also speak not with words which human wisdom can teach, but with words which the Holy Spirit teaches, and judge spiritual matters spiritually" (1 Corinthians 2, verses 12–13); and further in the same book, Chapter 7, verse 40: "I also have the Spirit of **God**," etc. Thus, there should be no one in today’s Christendom (except for the free and impudent <term>atheists<gloss>Athei; a Latinized plural for those who deny the existence of God or divine providence.</gloss></term>) who would, with doubt—

## To the Reader.

The persecution and the death of all the prophets and apostles—whom **God** has ever sent and gifted with the spirit of His little pearl from the beginning of the world until this time—is something we see and feel today, just as the ancients experienced it. This is evidenced by the words of Christ when He cried "Woe!" over the teachers of Israel, His own people, such as the <term>Scribes<gloss>Schrifftgelehrten; experts in religious law and scripture.</gloss></term> and <term>Pharisees<gloss>Phariseer; a Jewish sect known for strict, outward observance of the law.</gloss></term>. They sought to justify themselves and claim they were innocent of the blood of the ancient prophets, saying in Matthew 23: *If we had lived in the days of our fathers, we would not have taken part with them in shedding the blood of the prophets.* Yet they were the children of those who murdered the prophets, and they decorated the graves of the prophets with false praise.

This, as has been said, is seen today in our own time, where the teaching of Christ and His apostles is preached elegantly and artfully according to the "letter" <note>This refers to a literal or intellectual understanding of scripture devoid of spiritual life.</note> amidst great churchly splendor. It is honored through many ceremonies and external statutes in **baptisms**, **confessions**, **Holy Communion**, singing, ringing of bells, organs, and pipes. However, in the Spirit, He remains unrecognized; in His power, He is denied; and in the flesh, He is scorned, mocked, and persecuted.

<summary>The author compares contemporary religious authorities to the biblical Pharisees, arguing that modern church ceremonies and "splendor" mask a continued persecution of the true spiritual message.</summary>
<keywords>persecution, prophets, Pharisees, Matthew 23, hypocrisy, church ceremonies, spiritual vs. literal, religious pomp</keywords>

countrymen / of good German stock / begotten of a Christian and undefiled marriage-bed / born into this world / and named **JACOB** (as a future <term>subduer<note>In the biblical tradition, Jacob is the "supplanter" who takes the birthright from his brother Esau. entrance into the hollow mountain / is not much to be wondered at : since (as in the little book by <term>Heinrich Kornman<note>A German lawyer and author (c. 1570–1627) known for writing about the supernatural and folklore.</note></term> / called the *Venus-Berg* / likewise in the writings of the much and far-traveled and experienced <term>Leonhard Thurnheisser<note>An influential miracle-doctor, alchemist, and goldsmith at the court of the Elector of Brandenburg.</note></term> / as well as in Hammelmann in the *Holstein Chronicle*, <term>Theophrastus Paracelsus<note>The famous Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of minerals in medicine.</note></term>, <term>Agricola<note>Georgius Agricola, often called the "father of mineralogy."</note></term>, <term>Mathesius<note>Johannes Mathesius, a theologian who wrote a famous series of "mining sermons."</note></term>, <term>Aldrovandi<note>Ulisse Aldrovandi, an Italian naturalist who compiled massive encyclopedias of the natural world.</note></term>, Theobald, <term>Kircher<note>Athanasius Kircher, a Jesuit polymath who wrote "Mundus Subterraneus" (The Subterranean World).</note></term>, Zeiller, and others : likewise contained in the *Wahlen-Schatz* and *Mining Booklets*) such wonder-places have been encountered here and there. Indeed, they are found on the <term>Giant Mountains<note>The Riesengebirge, a mountain range along the modern border of the Czech Republic and Poland.</note></term> / near the Hirschberg warm springs in Silesia / especially at the <term>Aventrot Castle<note>Likely referring to a local Silesian fortification or ruin associated with legends of hidden treasure.</note></term> / under the stone with seven corners / and many other places. Yes, the pious and learned though little-known man <term>Johan Beer of Schweidnitz<note>A local mystic and figure in Silesian lore known for his visions.</note></term> in the year 1570 / by Divine permission / went so far that at certain times he could go into the <term>Zotten<note>The Zottenberg (Mount Ślęża), a mountain in Silesia famous for its ancient cult sites and mystical legends.</note></term> and other mountains situated thereabouts (yet according to a certain measure / and not without Divine fear) / and see the wonders and treasures of the Earth therein / and use them according to necessity. [As may be heard in detail in the little book printed a few years ago in Amsterdam regarding the *Gain and Loss of Spiritual and Bodily Goods* : as well as in the remarkable account of the three banished spirits in the Zotten Mountain (with whom the honorably mentioned Johan Beer held personal conversation).]

6. Now we turn back again to our **Jacob** : Whose parents, because they noticed / that a very fine, good, and spiritual nature had shown itself in this their son / they kept him at school /

# Jacob Böhme’s [Life]

maintained / where besides daily prayer / he also learned to read and write a little as was necessary / in accordance with the customary discipline of the table and home / until they <note>his parents</note> put him to the **shoemaker’s trade** ; in which he completed his training honestly and honorably / thereafter he traveled as a <term>journeyman<note>A trained craftsman who has completed an apprenticeship but is not yet a master; traditionally, they spent years traveling to different masters to refine their skills.</note></term> / and finally in the year 1594 / became at once a master and a bridegroom / with the virtuous maiden **Catharina** / the biological daughter of the honorable **Hans Kunschman** / a citizen and butcher in **Görlitz** <note>A prominent trading city on the border of Silesia and Upper Lusatia, where Böhme lived most of his life.</note>. With her, he lived for 30 years until his blessed end / in a quiet and peaceful marriage / and through God's blessing fathered 4 sons / of whom one became a goldsmith / the other a shoemaker / and the others became other craftsmen.

7. Accordingly, our well-remembered **J. B.** <note>Jacob Böhme</note> was from youth onward devoted to the fear of God in all humility and simplicity / and especially enjoyed attending sermons : he was finally awakened within himself through the comforting promise of our Savior in **Luke 11, verse 13**:
> The Father in heaven will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him for it /
as he was simultaneously stirred and moved by the strife / and the manifold **scholastic bickering** <note>original: "Schul-gezäncke." This refers to the rigid and often hostile theological disputes among university-trained academics and clergy that Böhme found alienating.</note> concerning religion (to which he could not adapt or reconcile himself) / so that in order to recognize the truth / yet in simplicity of spirit / he prayed, sought, and knocked fervently and incessantly / until (at that time while with his master during his travels) he was / through the drawing of the Father in the Son / transported according to the spirit into the **Holy Sabbath** and the heartfelt day of rest for the soul / and thus his request was granted ; where (according to his own **confession**) he was surrounded by Divine Light /

[8. In these truly **Apocalyptic** <note>Relating to the uncovering of hidden divine secrets; at this time, "Apocalyptic" referred to the tradition of revealing spiritual mysteries rather than just the end of the world.</note> schools of the Spirit of God—which are now rejected out of great blindness and malice—the holy patriarchs, kings, prophets, apostles, and men of God have studied at all times. From there, they have revealed the mystery of the Kingdom and the Judgment of **GOD** and **Christ** (just as Christ, the eternal Wisdom of the Father, did Himself) through all kinds of parables and figures, high and deep sayings and speeches, as well as through wonders and deeds before the world; and they have announced these things most earnestly and steadfastly through the offering up of their own bodies and lives.]

9. And it may well be that from the outside, through the <term>Magical-Astral<note>In Böhme’s worldview, this refers to the influence of the stars and celestial spirits upon the physical world, acting as a bridge between the spiritual and material realms.</note></term> influence of the starry spirits, a hidden ember and tinder was added to this holy fire of love. For as the blessed man himself told me, it happened once during his years of apprenticeship that a stranger—poorly dressed, yet a refined and honorable man—came to the shop and desired to buy a pair of shoes. Because neither the master nor the mistress was at home, **J. B.** <note>Jacob Böhme</note>, as an apprentice, did not dare to sell them until the man earnestly insisted upon it. And when he offered the shoes at a rather high price (intending to discourage the buyer and exceeding what was fair), the man gave him that very amount of money immediately and without any objection, took the shoes, went on his way, and when he had gone a little distance from the shop, [he stood] still...

# [The Life of] Jacob Böhme

...stood and called with a loud and earnest voice:
> Jacob, come out!

At which he was terrified within himself, that this unknown man called him by his own <term>baptismal name</term><note>The specific name given to a person at their Christian baptism; Böhme was shocked that a total stranger already knew it.</note>, yet he recovered himself, stood up, and went out to him in the street. There the man, who had an appearance both earnest and friendly with eyes sparkling with light, took him by the right hand, looked him straight and hard in the eyes and said:
> **Jacob, you are small, but you shall become great and a very different kind of person and man, so that the world will marvel at you! Therefore, be pious, fear God, and honor His word; especially, read gladly in <term>Holy Scripture</term><note>The Bible; "H. Schrifft" is an abbreviation for "Heilige Schrift."</note>, wherein you have comfort and instruction; for you will have to suffer much distress and poverty, along with persecution; but be of good cheer and remain steadfast, for you are dear to God, and He is gracious to you!**

Whereupon the man pressed his hand, looked him hard in the eyes again, and so went his way. Jacob, however, was not a little startled by this, and kept such <term>prophecy</term><note>A divinely inspired prediction of the future.</note> and admonition, along with the appearance of the man, always in his mind and could not forget it. Henceforth, he became more earnest and attentive in all his doings, so that the aforementioned spiritual outcry and <term>Sabbath day</term><note>Referring to the day of rest and the spiritual "Sabbath" or peace Böhme experienced during his mystical awakening.</note> followed a short time thereafter.

10. From which, when he had come to himself again, he subsequently laid aside the lusts of foolish youth more and more, and diligently went to church, along with...

## Biography

Through the reading of the **Holy Bible**, and the regular use of the preached word and the most venerable <term>sacraments</term><note>Formal Christian rites, such as Baptism and Holy Communion, believed to be a means of divine grace.</note>, he was driven by Divine Zeal to such an extent that he could neither listen to nor tolerate shameful words and foolish talk—especially blasphemous speeches and curses. He even found it necessary to rebuke and correct his own master, for whom he worked. Thus, out of love for true godliness and virtue, he devoted himself to an honorable, secluded life and withdrew from all vanity and evil company. Because this was completely contrary to worldly custom, he was persecuted with mocking laughter and words of disgrace, and finally dismissed by his own master—who could not tolerate such a <term>house-prophet</term><note>A derisive nickname for someone who acts like a self-appointed preacher or moral guide within a household or workshop.</note>—to travel elsewhere as a <term>journeyman</term><note>A trained worker who has completed an apprenticeship but is not yet a master; "wandern" refers to the traditional travels of tradesmen.</note>.

11. Meanwhile, after he had supported himself as a faithful worker by the sweat of his brow, he was—at the beginning of the 17th <term>century</term><note>original: *Seculi*</note>, namely 1600, in the 25th year of his age—gripped a second time by the Divine Light. Through a sudden glance at a pewter vessel (as of a lovely <term>Jovial luster</term><note>A reference to the bright, clear, and "joyful" influence of the planet Jupiter in alchemy and astrology; Böhme saw a divine reflection in the metal's shine.</note>), he was led with his <term>astral soul-spirit</term><note>The "gestirnten Seelen-Geiste," referring to the part of the human spirit Böhme believed was connected to the stars and the macrocosm.</note> into the innermost ground or <term>center</term><note>original: *Centro*</note> of secret nature! At that moment, being somewhat doubtful and wishing to drive such perceived fantasies from his mind, he went out into the greenery at **Görlitz** before the **Neistor**<note>The "Neißtor" or Neisse Gate, a landmark in the city of Görlitz.</note> (near where he had his dwelling by the bridge). Nevertheless, he felt this received vision more and more clearly the longer he went on, so that by means of the imprinted <term>signatures</term><note>The "Signature of All Things" (Signatura Rerum), Böhme’s famous doctrine that the external appearance of every creature reveals its internal, spiritual essence.</note> or figures, features, and colors, he looked, as it were, into the heart of all creatures...

<meta>This page continues the biography of Jacob Böhme, following his second mystical awakening in 1600. It describes his quiet life following that event and his third, definitive spiritual experience in 1610, which led to the writing of his first book.</meta>

->**Jacob Böhme’s**<-

and could see into the innermost nature (as is also sufficiently clarified and contained in his booklet **The Signature of All Things**<note>original Latin: *de Signatura Rerum*; Böhme’s famous work on how the internal essence of things is revealed in their external form.</note>, which was impressed as a foundation within him). Because of this, he was showered with great joys; he remained silent, praised God, attended to his household affairs and the upbringing of his children, and treated everyone in a peaceful and friendly manner. Regarding this light he had received and his inner walking with God and Nature, he shared little or nothing with anyone.

12. But afterward, according to the hidden, working holy counsel and will of God, he was touched by God for a third time after ten years—namely in 1610—through the overshadowing of the Holy Spirit. He was graced and strengthened with new light and <term>right</term><note>Here meaning divine justice or the proper spiritual order of things.</note>. So that he would not let such great grace which had happened to him slip from his memory, nor resist his so holy and comforting Teacher <note>Referring to the Holy Spirit or the inner divine voice.</note>, he wrote (though only for himself), having only meager resources and provided with no books other than the **Holy Bible**.

13. In the year 1612, [he wrote] his first book, **The Morning Glow at Sunrise** (later named **AURORA**<note>Latin for "dawn" or "daybreak."</note> by Dr. Balthasar Walter). Although he entrusted this book to no one—until finally, upon great request, he gave it only for a reading to a well-known nobleman who happened to find it in his possession—he did not want it to come into the open light of day, much less be printed. Yet the nobleman, out of a great desire for such a <term>hidden foundation</term><note>A reference to the deep, esoteric spiritual truths Böhme claimed to uncover.</note>, immediately divided the manuscript and, in addition to his own hand, had it quite zealously copied out by various scribes day and night. Whereupon it [was passed] from one to the

# Biography

known to others, until it finally also became known to the **Senior Pastor**<note>German: *Ober-pfarrer*. The head clergyman of the city's main church.</note> of Görlitz, **Gregorius Richter**. Following the common or perverse "shoemaker’s custom"<note>This refers to the proverb "Cobbler, stick to your last," which Richter used to mock Böhme's trade as a shoemaker while implying he had no right to write theology.</note>, and without sufficient examination or understanding, he immediately condemned it with public blasphemy from the pulpit in the highest degree. He repeated and pursued this with personal insults and threats of banishment against the innocent author so often and for so long, until finally the **Council of Görlitz** itself made an inquiry. They summoned Jacob Böhme as their citizen before them, secured the book in the Town Hall, and warned the author to be content with his <term>cobbler’s last</term><note>A wooden model of a foot used by shoemakers; here used metaphorically to tell Böhme to stick to his trade.</note> and to leave the writing of books alone. How this process unfolded is found elsewhere in detail: specifically regarding the unseemly zeal of this premature judge, and the great gentleness and humility of the late J. B., alongside other highly significant testimonies from the **Electoral Saxon** theologians. This can be found explained at length in an attached <term>Epistle</term><note>A formal letter or written communication.</note> by **Dr. Cornelius Weissner**, a famous physician and chemist. The <term>Autograph</term><note>original: *Autographum*; the original manuscript written in the author's own hand.</note> or Jacob Böhme’s own handwriting of this book, after it had been in the Council's custody for 27 years, was presented on November 26, 1641, by **Dr. Paul Scipio**, the Mayor there, to the Electoral Saxon House-Marshal at Dresden, **Mr. Georg Pflug** (as a well-disposed patron of our J. B.) when he came to Görlitz. From his hand, it was further sent through H. P. H. to **Abraham Willems van Beyerland**, a citizen and merchant in Amsterdam.

[14. From which it can be seen how the **Prince of Darkness**<note>A common name for the Devil or Satan.</note>, as a sworn hereditary enemy of the true divine...

# Jacob Böhme

...of the divine light. Through the "mystery of wickedness"<note>original: *Geheimnüß der Boßheit*; a biblical reference to 2 Thessalonians 2:7, describing the hidden power of evil at work in the world.</note>—which reveals itself ever more clearly through its members and instruments—[the persecutor] sets himself quite outrageously, maliciously, and blindly against everything called God or Good. Indeed, he sets himself against and even above Christ—the essential, life-giving **Word** and the Lord God Himself—and does not rest until he has vented his spite and struck down the innocent. For this, he shall pay with eternal wrath and gnashing of teeth in the hellish flames of fire, never to behold the blessed light and lovely face of God in eternity! Woe to him and his children of hell, those slanderous and irreconcilable **viper-tongues** and **tiger-hearts**.]

15. Following this, the holy, patient man observed a complete <term>Sabbath</term><note>A period of rest or cessation from work; here referring to Böhme’s seven-year silence (1612–1619) during which he obeyed the city council's order to stop writing.</note> for seven whole years out of obedience to his **authorities**, and during that time, he wrote nothing. However, he was eventually strengthened and awakened by abundant grace through the "fourth movement"<note>In Böhme’s mystical theology, he described spiritual revelations as "movements" or "births" of the divine principle within the soul.</note> of the Divine foundation laid within him. He was also earnestly urged by several God-fearing people and those with an understanding of nature not to "bury" such a precious <term>talent</term><note>original: *Pfund oder Pfand*; literally a "pound" (currency) or a "pledge," referencing the biblical Parable of the Talents where servants are judged for how they use what the master entrusted to them.</note>, but to put it to good use for the honor of God and the benefit of His community. Therefore, in the name of God, he took up the pen once more. Having no <term>capital</term><note>original: *Verlag*; referring to the financial backing or stock needed to maintain his shoemaking business.</note> to continue his trade, he used his resulting leisure and peace to produce the following heartfelt and glorious writings, which shall endure until the end of the world.

[...to strike everything over one last <note>A metaphor from shoemaking (Böhme's trade), meaning to force different things into a single mold or standard</note> / and whatever does not perfectly align or agree in every part / to immediately brand as heresy and condemn / as can be found very thoroughly and understandably explained in these writings of our **Teutonicus** <note>The "Teutonic Philosopher," a title given to Böhme to honor his German heritage and wisdom</note>.]

20. For example, the little Greek word ***IDEA*** <note>original: *IDEA*; referring to the divine archetype or form</note> was particularly pleasing to him when I shared it / and as he called it / it was like a uniquely beautiful, heavenly, pure virgin / and a spiritually and physically exalted goddess.

21. chimes in very lovely and most edifyingly toward true Christian-believing <term>Gelassenheit<gloss>A central term in German mysticism meaning "yieldedness," "resignation," or "letting go" of the self-will to allow God to work within</gloss></term>; it also gives us to understand that in the right Unified Truth and eternal Wisdom—in, with, and before GOD the everywhere-present Essential Good—there is no second or contrary thing, but rather One with One, indeed all an Eternal, Inward and Unified One, as the supersensory and super-essential peace of GOD Himself!

# Jacob Böhme’s

parting, which took place on Sunday, November 17/27 <note>This double date reflects the difference between the Julian "Old Style" and Gregorian "New Style" calendars, which were both in use during this period in Germany</note>, after he had first called for his son Tobias and asked: Whether he also heard that beautiful music? > O you mighty **GOD of Sabaoth** <note>original: "GOTT Zebaoth." But when it was barely 6 o'clock in the morning, he took leave of his wife and son, blessed them, and then spoke: "Now I journey hence into Paradise!" He asked his son to help him turn over, sighed deeply, and fell asleep; thus, he departed from this world with peace, quite softly and quietly.

-># The Life Course<-

departure drew near: behold, to the utter surprise of those standing around, this holy man cried out loudly: "Oh, what do I hear? Or do I alone hear it? What is this voice? What is this lovely song?" As they wondered at this for a long time yet heard nothing, they realized that this man—beloved by God and admitted to Divine wonders and mysteries—was no longer living in a human or earthly manner, but in a heavenly way. He was now returning to his dwelling and eternal place of rest, which he had previously left and lost in Adam <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "rest lost in Adam" refers to the original spiritual state of humanity before the Fall, which the mystic regains through Christ.</note>. ]

31. Such a **Good Death with Immortality** <note>original Latin: "Euthanasiá cum Athanasiá," meaning a peaceful death that leads directly to eternal life</note>, or blessed vision and foretaste, was far more common and well-known among the pious and simple Christians of old than among our modern "world-fantasists," who are misled by mere outward pomp and loud blather. This is sufficiently proven elsewhere by the examples of the saints and blessed ones who have fallen asleep in **GOD**, their holy Sabbath and **Lord of Hosts** <note>original: "Zebaoth," the Hebrew "Sabaoth"</note>.

32. After this, the pale body of our blessed <term>J. B.</term> <note>Jacob Böhme</note> was properly cleansed and shrouded, subsequently placed in a coffin, and buried with an honorable and Christian **funeral procession** <note>original: "Conduct"</note>. Despite the opposition of the **head pastor** <note>original: "Ober-Prediger." This refers to Gregor Richter, Böhme’s long-time clerical antagonist in Görlitz, who continued to attack him even after death</note>—or "High Priest"—who slandered even the dead, the city council granted a funeral sermon and an honorable burial. He was laid to rest there in Görlitz at the <term>God’s Acre</term> <note>original: "Gottes-Acker," a traditional German term for a churchyard or cemetery</note> with the customary tolling of bells and singing. Later, the following monument or memorial of honor (sent from Silesia, though not long afterward defiled with filth and broken to pieces due to the instigation of devil-possessed slandering tongues) was placed and erected upon this grave.

# Jacob Böhme’s Grave

33. It was a black wooden cross featuring the Hebrew name **IHSVH** <note>original: "I H S V H." This is the Pentagrammaton, Böhme’s mystical spelling of Jesus’s name, where the Hebrew letter 'Shin' is inserted into the four-letter name of God, YHWH, to represent the Word becoming flesh.</note> and twelve golden sun-rays. Below this was a little child resting upon a skull, leaning on its arm and head <note>A common "memento mori" image representing the triumph of the soul’s innocence over death.</note>, inscribed with these eight letters: **V. H. I. L. I. C. I. V.** (as explained above) <note>These letters likely form an acronym used by Böhme’s circle, possibly standing for "Unser Heil im Leben in Christus ist Victory" or a similar devotional phrase in German or Latin.</note>.

34. Within a wide oval circle or field, the following words were written: "Born of **GOD**, died in **IHSVH**, sealed with the Holy Spirit, here rests **Jacob Böhme** of **Old Seidenburg** <note>Alt Seidenberg was Böhme's birthplace near Görlitz.</note>, who departed blessedly on November 7/17 at 6 o'clock in the morning in the 50th year of his age." <note>The double date 7/17 reflects the difference between the Julian and Gregorian calendars still in use at the time.</note>

35. To the right, toward the south, a black eagle was painted on the cross atop a high mountain; its left leg stepped upon the head of a large coiled snake, while in its right it held a palm branch. With its beak, it received a lily branch offered out of the Sun. Next to this stood, quite appropriately, **I HAVE SEEN** <note>original Latin: "VIDI"</note>.

36. To the left of the cross, toward the north, stood a lion crowned with a golden crown and cross. Its right hind foot rested upon a squared cornerstone or **Cube** <note>original Latin: "Cubo"</note>, while its left rested upon an overturned **Imperial Orb** or **Globe** <note>original Latin: "Globo"</note>. In its right front paw, it held a fire-flaming sword, and in its left a burning heart, where the word **I HAVE CONQUERED** <note>original Latin: "VICI"</note> was skillfully placed.

37. In the middle, however, beneath the broad oval rhyme of the epitaph on the stem of the cross, stood a Lamb wearing a bishop’s hat and **Miter** <note>original: "Inful," a traditional liturgical headdress</note>, similar to those found in the 29th of the 32 magical figures <note>These "magical figures" refer to the *Wunder-Figuren* (Wonder-Figures), a series of mystical diagrams illustrating Böhme's complex spiritual system.</note>.

## Biography.

<note>The following passage refers to a vision or symbolic representation involving Paracelsus, a famous 16th-century physician and alchemist who heavily influenced Böhme’s thought.</note>
Aureolus Theophrastus Paracelsus is to be found under a palm tree by a bubbling spring, grazing upon a green meadow among the flowers, where it stood: **I CAME**; and these three words are to be understood from the single word **Christ** in the following manner:

> I CAME into the world! I SAW Satan descend!
> I CONQUERED Hell! LIVE, you great-souled ones.
> <note>original Latin: "In Mundum VENI! Sathanam descendere VIDI! Infernum VICI! VIVITE Magnanimi." This is a Christian adaptation of Julius Caesar's famous "Veni, Vidi, Vici."</note>

38. Lastly, at the bottom near the earth, rising up the stem of the cross, were his final words:

> Now I travel hence into Paradise,
> There he gives God praise and glory:
> We look after, and wait,
> Until we also end our course;
> Lord Jesus come, gather us all home.

39. And so much for the simple biography of the God-taught and in-God-blessedly-resting German Wonder-Man <term>Jacob Böhme</term> <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a humble shoemaker turned Christian mystic whose complex writings on the nature of God and evil gained a massive following.</note>—at least as much as was necessary to know from his own mouth and the brief reports of the friends who were present, excluding what might have been noted by others or perhaps forgotten and omitted by myself.

40. But should there now be someone who is offended either by the simple person or by the highly-worthy gift of the author, and might suspect that someone else was "hiding under the covers," concealing themselves under such a name to prattle something new and unknown to the curious world—or even perhaps some old, damned heresy and...

A reference to Acts 9:15, where Paul is called a "chosen vessel" to carry God's name.</note> likewise, out of a poor, lame shoemaker—as occurred under **Julian the Apostate** <note>Roman Emperor (r. 361–363 AD) who rejected Christianity.</note>—He made a petitioner and miracle-worker (who through his prayer had to move a mountain into the sea); or as happened a few years ago in Wittmund in East Frisia, awakening a man wise in the Scriptures (of whom **Johann Angelus Werdenhagen** <note>A German philosopher and diplomat (1581–1652) who wrote about Böhme.</note> writes in *Psychology* <note>original: "Psychologia."</note> page 365); and in summary, to make something out of nothing—indeed, to make everything into whatever He Himself wills.

43. Or does the **Almighty** not have the power to do what He wishes with His own? Do you, proud Pharisee, envious High Priest, and meddling scribe, look so sour and squint-eyed because the **LORD**, our merciful **GOD** and Father, is so pious and kind toward His children? If so, go then and, out of heartbreak from your own anger and pride, bite off your own tongue with gnashing teeth; and tear and devour your own envious and godless heart out of your body with fierce and hellish resentment. Thus it can be recognized that your birth is from the ancient serpent and the vengeful pits of hell, and all your skill is from the proud **Lucifer** and the angry dragon-devil; but by no means from God in **Christ**, and His Holy Spirit and word of grace and truth.

44. Or should it not be necessary, in these current highly dangerous times, or for a devastated and destroyed Christendom, that **GOD** should finally look upon it and speak with other lips to these misled peoples and their blind leaders? For all flesh corrupts its way before the **Lord**, and especially the Evil One in his so-called spiritual and Christian—

45. He who has an ear to hear, or an eye to see, let him hear and see what the Word and Light of Truth says and shows through its witnesses, who are called and chosen for this purpose. He will find that both Heaven and Earth wage war against the current generation of men, and that another **Time and Birth** is at hand. This will finally reveal itself over all the world in an instant <note>original: "in einem Nun," meaning in a single moment or a "Now."</note>, like lightning from the rising to the setting of the sun, bringing with it the seventh **Fire-Evening** and the final **Day of Judgment** or **Sabbath-Day**. This is what the holy Prophets, Apostles, and other enlightened men of God have always seen in the spirit, and have proclaimed to the final world from the very beginning. Therefore, blessed is the servant who is awake when his Master comes!

46. In summary, let no one stumble against this **Cornerstone of Simplicity** <note>original: "Eck-stein der Einfalt." A reference to Christ as the cornerstone, emphasizing that divine wisdom is found in humble, simple faith rather than intellectual pride.</note>, lest he be shattered by it. Instead, let him be raised up by it and consider that Heaven is highest where the Earth is low-

# Life-Path.

...most terrifying; and that according to the Word of the **Lord**, every hill and high mountain must be made low and the valleys must be exalted, so that it may be equal and level everywhere, and one may henceforth walk without stumbling in the Land of the Living. <note>A reference to Isaiah 40:4, a common theme in Böhme’s work representing the leveling of human pride to prepare for divine grace.</note>

47. Praised be the **LORD**, the Most High, who humbles this one and exalts that one, and gives His Spirit when and to whom He will, so that no flesh may boast before Him.

48. Concerning specifically the entrusted talent <note>original: "Pfund," literally "pound," referring to the Parable of the Talents in Luke 19:11-27.</note>, as the gift of grace <note>original: "Genaden-Gabe."</note>, which **God**, the highest and only Giver of all good, has placed from above into this earthen vessel <note>original: "irdene Gefäße," a reference to 2 Corinthians 4:7, emphasizing the humble human container of divine wisdom.</note>—which is despised by the clever and proud world—as a great treasure and precious fine pearl: it is of such dignity and goodness that, in my opinion (if one indeed has open windows toward Jerusalem <note>An allusion to Daniel 6:10, meaning a heart and mind directed toward God's truth despite worldly distraction.</note>), since the time of the Apostles, hardly such a high and deep foundation of the essential knowledge of the most holy Divine **Trinity**, and the light of hidden and manifest Nature, Grace, and Majesty, has been opened and shown to the people of this earth.

49. There it is especially to be considered why **God** granted and opened such a high gift of grace specifically to our ruined fatherland of the **High German Nation**, which is devastated both spiritually and physically; especially through such a lowly and unimposing person <note>Böhme refers to himself, a simple shoemaker, as the "unimposing person" chosen for this revelation.</note>, and in such a confused time; when everything is sunk and dead in deep slumber, slime, and the sorrow of temporal cares, lust, and sustenance (as if in death), so that one even [neglects/forgets] the eternal invisible kingdom of grace and heaven of **God** and

world / and of the kind Word of God in us) as well; as the author of these heartfelt writings testifies and indicates very clearly and extensively here and there.

53. Yes, even the men illuminated by **God** in the universal or general main foundation of the eternally-weaving and living Word—among the heathens, Jews, and Christians—studied and practiced their <term>oracles</term> <note>original: *oracula*</note>, <term>breathings</term> <note>original: *spiracula*; refers to divine inspirations or the "breath of life"</note>, and <term>miracles</term> <note>original: *miracula*</note> (or wonders, words, works, and deeds) in a much different way than the current <term>synagogues</term> and <term>high places</term> <note>a biblical reference to sites of unauthorized or idolatrous worship</note> in <term>Babel</term> <note>Böhme’s term for the "city of confusion," representing the corrupt and divided institutional churches of his time.</note> and Israel (especially the formerly self-pious, clever, seeing, and wealthy Lady **Self-Love** <note>original: **Philautia**; a personification of the Greek word for self-love, which Böhme considers the root of spiritual blindness.</note> at **Laodicea** <note>A reference to the church in the Book of Revelation (3:14-22) that considered itself wealthy and successful but was spiritually "lukewarm" and blind.</note>) are willing to hear, see, know, or believe and accept—even if **God** Himself lets it echo and be painted before their ears and eyes loudly and strongly enough that they could almost grasp it with their hands.

54. Of this we could, with the help of God, provide a well-attested introduction and explanation, if it were appropriate or necessary for this place. However, in *The Starry Eye* <note>original: *Oculo Sydereo*</note>, *The Mystical Trinity* <note>original: *Triade Mystica*</note>, *The Way of the Ancient Sages* <note>original: *via veterum Sapientum*</note>, *The Gospel of the Exiles* <note>original: *Evangelio Exulantum*</note>, *Abundance* <note>original: *Copia*</note> of the Abomination of Desolation, *Theomantic Judgment* <note>original: *Judicio Theomantico*; "theomantic" refers to divination or judgment by the direct word of God</note>, *Sephiriel*, *Raphael*, and elsewhere, sufficient occasion is given for further exploration of the true **Theosophical foundation**; just as other authors, new and old, have been cited alongside **Holy Scripture** (especially in *The Starry Eye*), in whose works a thorough and detailed account may be found.

55. One might also [consider] that which is mentioned under No. 17 of the highly esteemed, pious, and Christian jurist and statesman **J. Ang. <insert>Werdenhagen's True Psychology</insert>** <note>original: **Werdenhagens Psychologiam veram**; Johannes Angelius Werdenhagen was a 17th-century scholar who attempted to reconcile Böhme’s mysticism with political science and the study of the soul (*psychology*).</note>

->J. B. T.<-

# Course of Life
5

If one were to read J. B. T. <note>Likely referring to Jacob Böhme Teutonicus</note> with particular diligence, one would find this theme and primary subject of the <term>God-taught</term> <note>original: *Theodidactis*; those who receive wisdom directly from God rather than through human schooling</note>—as opposed to the <term>world-taught</term> <note>original: *Cosmodidactos*; those whose knowledge is limited to worldly or academic tradition</note>—fully discussed and executed with the help of Holy Scripture, Dr. Luther, and other highly learned men. It distinguishes between the Spirit of **God** and the spirit of this world, showing how the friendship and wisdom of this world are nothing but enmity and foolishness before **God**. Finally, it shows how, through the deceit and cunning of the ancient serpent, such worldly wisdom brings death, judgment, and eternal damnation. This is especially evident where he, in the dedication to ten noble <term>statesmen</term> <note>original: *Politicos*; civil authorities or public officials</note> (who were almost more like "bloody judges" than some named <term>theologians</term>), mentions our <term>Teuton</term> <note>original: *Teutonici*; referring to Jacob Böhme, the "Teutonic Philosopher"</note> with special honor in section Lit. a. 6. c. 5. d. 3. and on pages 63, 75, 365, 548, and 604.

56. There are also other good writings available for the lovers of Divine Wisdom or true spiritual scholarship. One such work is the <term>Harmony</term> <note>original: *Harmonia*</note> or <term>Concordance</term> <note>original: *Concordanz*</note> of those whom the world now calls Catholic, Lutheran, and Calvinist or Reformed, printed in Augsburg in 1613. If one were to listen to these, one would find enough testimony of such fundamental schools within their own writings. However, their current lukewarm and lazy successors and students know nothing of this anymore, even though they still want to carry and claim those names. They are like the Jews, who boast of Abraham's seed and name but do not possess Abraham's faith, life, or works. It does no good to decorate and beautify the graves of the deceased saints and prophets so handsomely on the outside, if on the inside everything tastes <note>original: *schmeckt*; the catchword for the next page</note> only of death and hell.

# Jacob Böhme’s

## True Account by Mr. Cornelius Weißner, Doctor of Medicine, regarding the gentleness, humility, and friendliness of the late Jacob Böhme. Also concerning the examination in Dresden, in the presence of his Electoral Highness and eight of the most distinguished professors, etc.

->God’s grace, along with brotherly love and faithfulness, first and foremost!<-

Regarding the news and testimony of the late Jacob Böhme, <term>the Teutonic</term> <note>original: *Teutonici*; a Latinized nickname for Böhme, often called "Philosophus Teutonicus" or the "Teutonic Philosopher" because he wrote in German rather than Latin.</note> I am indeed—God knows—willing and inclined to do so, though I know little of it from my own experience; yet as much as is known to me, I write to your Charity <note>original: *E. L.* (Euer Liebden); a formal, affectionate form of address used in the 17th century between friends or social equals, literally "Your Love."</note> most heartily, and recognize myself to be indebted for much more than this.

My acquaintance with the aforementioned late J. B. began around July 1618, in Lauben in my fatherland, through a merchant and tailor (now resting in God, the late Libortus Schneller). He, along with his wife’s brother—a young preacher named Salomon Schröter, both of whom were good friends of the late J. B. and also of mine—were Böhme's loving and faithful <term>disciples</term> <note>original: *Discipulos*</note> who applied themselves very diligently to his writings, and had prayed for and obtained a praiseworthy, good understanding of them from GOD. I, however, had been the <term>tutor</term> <note>original: *Præceptor*; a private teacher or instructor</note> to the children of the nobleman near Schweidnitz, named Balthasar Tilkens.

[had been] and there, from him, I had conceived a contrary mind <note>original: *contrariam mentem*</note> (because he was the late J. I believed that he was stuck in the delusion of the Reformed <note>This refers to the "Reformed" or Calvinist branch of Protestantism, which Lutherans of the time often viewed as heretical, particularly regarding the doctrine of predestination.</note> regarding the **election of grace** <note>original: *Genaden-wahl*; the theological concept of how God chooses those who will be saved.</note> of God in His Son, etc. Therefore, the two aforementioned friends—when the late, dear man once came to them in Lauben—invited me to join them and provided the occasion and opportunity for a **Christian conference** <note>A formal but friendly religious discussion.</note>. This (praise and thanks be to the dear God for it) ended so blessedly that we became friends and, with a rightful setting aside of all suspicion and error, became one in Christian brotherly love. There, the late man endured my (then-held academic impetuousness) with highly astonishing and great friendliness, and discoursed <note>original: *discuriret*</note> in such love that, for the sake of the fear of God within me, I could no longer resist him, but had to surrender to the truth and the friendliness of the Spirit of Jesus Christ in him. Since that time, I have neither spoken with him nor seen him again.

But as for the **Görlitz Affair** <note>original: *Actum Görlicensem*; this refers to the public persecution Böhme faced from the local church authorities in his hometown of Görlitz.</note> which I recently recounted to Your Charity <note>original: *E. B.’s brother-in-law (a young baker who had recently married the late J. B.’s blood-relation) one **thaler** <note>A standard silver coin of the era.</note> for his necessities, to buy wheat for baking **Striezel** <term>Striezel</term> <note>A traditional German braided yeast bread, often associated with Christmas.</note> around Christmas time. In gratitude, the baker presented him with a rather large Striezel, and soon after the holidays, returned the one thaler...

brought and paid off, in the hope that the Pastor <note>original: *Prediger*; referring to Gregor Richter, the head pastor of Görlitz and Böhme’s chief antagonist.</note> would be satisfied with this interest for the sake of the Thaler <note>A large silver coin used as standard currency.</note>, which he had used for only two weeks. He himself would, on the young man’s behalf, satisfy <note>original: *contentiren*; to settle a debt or compensate.</note> him (the angry Pastor) regarding whatever further interest he desired for the sake of the borrowed Thaler, and would gladly bring it to him if he only knew how much the gentleman required. # **Life Story**

...had to trouble, molest, or disturb him? What did it concern him? He should mind his own business and be gone. He <note>Jacob Böhme.</note>, however, persisted and asked for grace, offering to set things right and to satisfy <term>satisfy (original: contentiren)</term><note>In this context, to satisfy a debt or provide financial compensation to someone's liking.</note> the gentleman. The gentleman <note>Pastor Gregor Richter.</note>, however, being ashamed of his own injustice and wrongdoing, would neither admit it nor say what he wanted, but instead once again ordered the petitioner or mediator <note>original: *Interponenten*; one who intercedes or steps in to resolve a conflict.</note> to be gone, pointing him to the room door through which he should clear out. 

He, the commanding gentleman, sat on his chair wearing slippers; and as the pious, sighing to God, humble, gentle, and very loving mediator turned away without having accomplished his task, and upon leaving the door, spoke a Christian farewell blessing ("God protect your Reverence" <note>original: *Ehrw.*, an abbreviation for *Ehrwürden*, a formal title for a member of the clergy.</note>) to the angry gentleman; the latter became enraged and, because of the blessing, behaved even more wickedly than before. He took the slipper from his foot and threw it after the late <note>original: *seel.* (blessed); a common term used to refer to a person who is now deceased.</note> man out the door, saying: "Why should you, you godless rascal, still bid me goodnight or wish me well? What do I care for your blessing?" etc. 

The dear man, however, remained unangered; he picked up the slipper, placed it back at his feet, and said: "Sir, do not be angry; I do you no harm; be commended to God!" and with that, he departed from him for that time.

# Jacob Böhme’s

...done to harm him? He [Böhme] answered that he did not know, nor could he learn it from him [the Pastor]. Therefore, he most humbly and diligently petitioned the most-wise Gentlemen of the City Council that they would please have the Lord Accuser or Preacher come and say what he had done to him?

Upon which the entire Council concluded it was only fair to kindly invite the Lord Preacher to them and require him to name the <term>grievances</term><note>original: *Gravamina*; formal legal complaints or grounds for a grievance.</note>. Thereupon, they sent two men of the Council <term>honorably</term><note>original: *honorificè*; with due respect or ceremony</note> to the Lord Preacher, asking him to come to them at the Town Hall, or to disclose the <term>specifics</term><note>original: *specificè*; in detail or specifically</note> of the grievances to the delegated Gentlemen!

At this, the Pastor became zealous and sent word asking what business he had in their court or Town Hall? Whatever he had to say, he said in God's stead from the <term>pulpit</term><note>original: *Canzel*</note>; that was his council chair and seat of profession. They should follow what he had said there and banish the frivolous, loose, and audacious heretic from the city, so that he might no longer resist the Holy Office of Preaching and bring the punishment of <term>Korah, Dathan, and Abiram</term><note>original: *Core, Dathan und Abiram*; Biblical figures from Numbers 16 who rebelled against Moses and were swallowed by the earth as divine punishment.</note> upon the entire city, etc.

Following this, the Gentlemen <term>deliberated</term><note>original: *consulirt*</note> and could not find how they might fairly remedy the matter, fearing the <term>vehemence</term><note>original: *Vehemens*; passion or forceful intensity</note> of their Preacher upon his pulpit. They decided to banish the innocent J. B. from the city. In this decision, several men of the Council would not consent, but rather stood up and left; the rest, however, <term>carried out the order</term><note>original: *exequiret*</note> and, through the court or city bailiffs, had the unconvicted, faithful citizen banished straight out through the city gate. To this, the patient and blessed man did not resist, but said:

# Life-Course
<meta>catchword: Writing</meta>

"In God’s name, gentlemen, I will do what you command / and absent myself from the city; but may I not first go into my house / and take my family with me / or at the very least speak a word of necessity with them?" They, however, turned him away and refused him / saying / they could not now change the judgment / which the entire Council had decided / in which he had heard that he was to be escorted straight from the Town Hall with shame and mockery out of the city. To this he said: "Yes, dear gentlemen / let it be done / since it cannot be otherwise / I am content." Thus, he was banished / and remained away overnight.

The following morning, however, when the Council met again / and had settled their disagreement / another conclusion was reached. They pursued the banished, innocent man / and sent word throughout the countryside / searching / and finally finding him / and he was **solemnly** led back into the city with honors; which was a miracle from **GOD** in the midst of these devilish acts and decrees.

This much is what I have been certainly informed of / that it all happened in this way. 
Regarding the **Proceedings**<note>original: *Actus*; refers to the formal legal or academic hearing.</note> at Dresden / I also know how to affirm it with certainty / though only <note>original: *authoritate aliorum fide dignorum & exceptione majorum*; by the authority of others worthy of belief and beyond reproach</note> that the blessed man of God—who was chased in and out like a hunted hind—was summoned to Dresden / and examined in the presence of his <term>Electoral Highness</term><note>The Prince-Elector of Saxony, Böhme's sovereign.</note> by a gathering of distinguished *Doctors* (named Dr. Hoë, Dr. Meisner, Dr. Baldwin, Dr. Gerhard, Dr. Leyser, and one other doctor whom I cannot name at this moment / as well as two *Professors of Mathematics*) / and of his...

<meta>This page continues from the introductory letter by Dr. Tobias Kober to the von Schweinichen family. It begins a detailed medical and personal account of the final days of the mystic Jacob Böhme in November 1624.</meta>

<header>Course of Life.</header>
<meta>catchword: Him</meta>

# Course of Life.

Along with the offering of my willing services, I wish for all blessed and temporal prosperity from **GOD**; and I cannot, out of Christian condolence, omit reporting to you how it occurred and transpired with our Christian brother and man of God, Jacob Böhme, here in Görlitz. For when he arrived here from Schweinhaus<note>The ancestral estate of the von Schweinichen family in Silesia where Böhme had been staying.</note> on Thursday fourteen days ago, the 7th of November, very ill and weak with great swelling and fatigue, I soon saw from all the circumstances that he would not live long. Thereupon, I also soon persuaded Mr. Melchior Bernt from Zittau to come down, who concluded with me in one mind that it had gone too far, either <term>due to a non-compliant patient</term><note>original: *causa ægri immorigeri*</note> or <term>by a movement of nature tending toward a predestined death</term><note>original: *naturæ motu ad mortem prædestinatam tendente*</note>.

And nothing more could be done (because <term>diarrhea</term><note>original: *alvi fluxus*</note>, <term>abdominal rumbling</term><note>original: *Rugitus ventris*</note>, <term>stabbing pains on the left side</term><note>original: *dolores lancinantes lateris sinistri*</note>, <term>swelling of the belly and feet</term><note>original: *excrescentia ventris & pedum*</note>, <term>tightness of the chest</term><note>original: *Angustia pectoris*</note>, <term>a gaping mouth</term><note>original: *hians os*</note>, <term>dryness</term><note>original: *siccitas*</note>, <term>extreme wasting of the chest and face</term><note>original: *consumptio summa thoracis & faciei*</note>, and <term>reddish urine with a black ring</term><note>original: *Urina ruffa circulo nigro*—in 17th-century uroscopy, a black circle in the urine was often seen as a sign of impending death.</note> (which was always thus) were present almost from the beginning to the end) than <term>strengthening remedies</term><note>original: *confortantia*</note>, which we did not let be lacking. It also caused us wonder that the current <term>Chemist</term><note>original: *Chymicus*; likely an apothecary or alchemist at the Schweinhaus estate.</note>—seeing as the patient, during the entire time he was at Schweinhaus, had enjoyed no substantial meat—had not finally provided him with potent <term>distillates</term><note>original: *Destillata*</note> and <term>extracts</term><note>original: *Extracta*</note> from the same for the journey. We have, therefore, looked after the dear, late Jacob as much as was possible.

And when we could perceive no rescue, and he became weaker from day to day, I and Christoff Ritter from Sprottau decided that one [should...]

<header>Jacob Böhme's</header>
<meta>catchword: us</meta>

...Him, so that he might be buried here in his homeland in the customary manner without offense (which had proceeded strangely)<note>Kober alludes to the controversy surrounding Böhme, who had been accused of heresy by the local clergy. Ensuring a "customary burial" required Böhme to prove his orthodoxy through confession and communion.</note>, it was mentioned to him to receive the **Lord's Supper** <note>original: *Cœnam Domini*</note>. When we informed him that his time might not last long and that **GOD** might take him from us, and that he should reconcile with everyone and receive **Communion** <note>original: *Communiciren*</note>, he promised to do so and was willing to put it into action with God's help. He desired this from Master Elias Theodor<note>Elias Theodor was a member of the local clergy in Görlitz who agreed to examine Böhme's faith before granting him the sacrament.</note>, whom I informed of the matter that evening. I also reminded the Master to raise several **questions, albeit difficult ones** <note>original: *Quæstiones licet difficiles*</note>, with him; to which he answered me with the following **note** <note>original: *Schedula*</note> (Letter A). When the Master was called on the morning of November 15th, he appeared before him and, **while we were absent** <note>original: *nobis absentibus*</note>, spoke all kinds of things with him and raised simple **questions** <note>original: *Quæstiones*</note> (Letter B)<note>The text uses "schlechte," which in 17th-century German meant "plain" or "straightforward" rather than "bad."</note>. And being satisfied with him, he administered the **Lord's Supper** to him. Thereupon he promised him that he would include him in his prayers and visit him further.

Now that this was performed in the name of **GOD**, he became weaker and weaker, yet remained in his right mind and did not concern himself much further with **earthly matters** <note>original: *terrestria*</note>. The following Saturday, in the presence of myself, Mr. Hans Rohte, Mr. Michael Kurtz, and his family, I indicated to him the peril of his life and the proximity of death; to which he answered: "In three days you will see how **GOD** has finished with me." And when we asked him whether he also wished to die, he answered: "Yes, according to **GOD's** will." Thereupon we commended him to **GOD** and wished that we would find him tomorrow, **GOD** willing, better than now. To which he answered: "May that help..."
